AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

avplcent Ea

Case Number: 04-12-2006-121358-020 Expiry: 03/01/2007

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

Copies of all reports UAP and associated correspondence for the period 1*
November — 31° December 1995.

Case for release of information
Enclosed are UFO reports from the requested time span.

Names and addresses were removed in accordance with exemption S.40
Personal Information of the Freedom of Information Act 2000.

Authorisation

| hereby give authorisation for the release of the aforementioned information
to the Applicant.
Reaction 40

Directorate of Air Staff - Freedom of Information 1

MINISTRY OF DEFENCE
5" Floor, Zone H, Main Building, Whitehall, London SW1A 2HB
Telephone (Direct dial) 020 7218 2140

(Switchboard) 020 7218 9000
(Fax)

Your Reference:
Our Reference:
Liverpool 04-12-2006-121358-020

Merseyside Date:
Section 40} 15 February 2007

pi ocction 40 |

I am writing concerning your Freedom of Information request regarding copies of all UFO/UAP
reports and associated correspondence for the period 1 November — 31 December 1995. Your
request has been passed to this Department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of
Defence for correspondence regarding UFOs. I apologise for the delay in replying.

I have enclosed for your information, copies of UFO reports for the time span 1 November — 31
December 1995. The names and addresses have been removed in accordance with exemption S.40
(Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000..Some security classifications
have also been removed. We are currently attempting to retrieve another file from our archives
and will write to you again if the file becomes available, and has any relevant information
regarding your request contained within it.

If you are unhappy with the response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not possible
and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by contacting
the Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB
(e-mail InfoXD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must be made
within two calendar months of the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has
come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate the case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely

ENCLOSURE TRANSFERRED TO
FILE D/DAS/10/2/8/16 PART G
Tam writing concerning your Freedom of Information request regarding UFO sightings in
Northumberland since 2001. Your request has been passed to this Department as we are the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence regarding UFOs. | apologise for
the delay in replying.

First, it may be heipful if | explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have
some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s
airspace might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is
evidence of a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no
‘UFO’ report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of
each sighting reported to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, could be found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it
is not the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be
an inappropriate use of defence resources if we were to do so.

With regard to the records the MOD holds of reported UFO sightings, | should inform you that
they are not held electronically but are filed on paper files in the order in which they are
received and we currently hold records spanning a 25 year period. They are not segregated
by geographical area. In order to identify records specifically from Northumberland, a manual
search of thousands of records would be required and this would exceed the £600 cost limit
set for compliance with the FOI Act 2000. However, we are currently in the process of
compiling a database of UFO sighting reports and this has now been campleted for the years
2002, 2003, 2004, 2005 and to date in 2006. | examined these records, but found no UFO
sighting reports for Northumberland for those years.

In the meantime, you may wish to be aware that there is some information about UFO’s
available for public viewing. MOD files were routinely destroyed after 5 years until 1967 when
they were generally preserved for The National Archives. A few have survived before 1967
and these together with records up to 1977 are now available for public viewing. The National
Archives can be.contacted at Ruskin Avenue, Kew, Richmond, Surrey TW9 4DU or telephone,
020 8876 3444. The National Archives also have a website giving information about the
records they hold and how to access them. This can be found on the internet at:

http://www. nationalarchives.gov.uk. The Ministry of Defence Freedom of Information website
also contains some released information on UFOs. This can be accessed via the internet at:

htto://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/EreedomOfInformation/PublicationScheme.

| hope this is helpful. If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any
aspect of the handling of this request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first
instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by
contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall,
SW1A 2HB (e.mail: InfoXD @mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may be able to take your complaint to
the Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of
Information Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate
your case until the MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the
role and powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's

website, htto://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely

Ministry of Defence

Directorate of Air Staff — Freedom of Information 1
5" Floor, Zone H

Main Building

London
SW1A 2HB

AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

Applicant: SEIS

Case Number: 10-07-2006-170158-096 Expiry: 10/07/2006

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

We have received reports of a UFO from a reader which he captured on his
cam-corder at about 7pm on Thursday 8 June.

The object was visible for about eight minutes and is described as slowly
moving up into the sky at an angle.

He stood in Banbury and saw the object to the southeast (Brackley direction).

Was wondering if:

You can identify the object from the pics (see below)
What military activity there is in the area which could possibly explain it (saw a
news article on the TV the other day about an Apache helicopter which did
look a bit like this)
If Upper Heyford airbase (which is in that direction) is ever used for military
activity (I believe it was is supposed to have been closed)
You could give us a general comment about the regularity of UFO sightings,
particularly in this area, and how the majority of sightings are
explained/resolved (if indeed they are?). Plus historica data e.g. are sightings

. becoming less or more common?

Case for release of information

| have informed Eee of our Policy on UFOs and that we do not
investigate sightings.

| have told him that Upper Heyford was closed in 1994 and has not been a
working base since then. So air activity would not have come from there.

| asked Low Flying if there had been any military activity in the area and they
informed me that there wasn’t on the date and time of the sighting.

| told ERIRMRAGI that we rarely get any UFO sightings from Banbury in Oxon
and that over the past few years, UFO sighting reports in general to this office
have dwindled.

Authorisation

| hereby give authorisation for the release of the aforementioned information
to the Applicant.

eporter

Banbury Guardian Our Reference:

7 North Bar 10-07-2006-170158-096

Banbury Date:

Oxon, OX16 0TQ 30 August 2006

Den

I am writing with reference to your enquiry to the MOD Press Office on the 27 June about a UFO
sighting on the 8 June 2006. Your request has been considered to be a request made in accordance
with the Freedom of Information Act and passed to this Department as we are the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence regarding UFOs. I apologise for the delay in sending this response.

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
‘unidentified flying objects’ it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom’s airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external source, and to date no ‘UFO’ report has
revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each sighting reported
to us. We believe that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be
found for them if resources were diverted for this purpose, but it is not the function of the MOD to
provide this kind of aerial identification service. It would be an inappropriate use of defence
resources if we were to do so.

You asked if Upper Heyford has been used for military activity since it has been closed. The base
was handed back to the MOD in 1994, and has not been a working base since then, so no military
activity would have come from there on the date of the sighting.

I have enquired as to if there was any military activity on the date of the UFO sighting, and have
been informed that there was no low flying, but there could have been military activity over the
area, but without checking every RAF base, I can not give you any details.

You commented on the regularity of UFO sightings in the Banbury, area. Generally, we very
rarely receive any sightings from this area, and this sighting report that you have forwarded to us,
has been the only one within the last five years. With regard to how many sighting reports we have
received in the UK, generally sighting reports have dwindled over the last few years. In the past
five years we have received only 703 sightings in the United Kingdom.

I hope this is helpful. If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any aspect
of the handling of this request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance.
Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the
Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-

mail: Info-XD@mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on _ the Commissioner’s website,

http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely

From: EERE

Sent: 13 November 2006 14:51

Subject: internet-Authorised: FOI REQUEST29-09-2006-143737-007

I am writing concerning your letter dated 3 June in which you make a Freedom of Information
request for information regarding UFOs. Firstly, please accept my apologies for not responding
sooner, the person that would normally deal with this matter has now moved to another branch and
her post remains vacant, which is causing a delay in dealing with correspondence.

The Ministry of Defence report entitled “Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence
Region” (otherwise known as the Condign report) has been placed on the MoD website at
http://www.mod.uk/Defencelnternet/FreedomofInformation/PublicationScheme/SearchPublicationSc
UkAirDefenceRegion.htm. There is no charge to view or download the document.

Turning to the rest of your request, it is not clear exactly what information you are seeking and I
would be grateful if you could clarify this before we are able to proceed. Before doing this please
bare in mind that the bulk of our files are stored on paper, which would require a manual search in
order to provide the information you requested. The cost limit set for compliance with the UK
Freedom of Information Act 2000 is £600 and as provided by Section 12 of the Act, if your request
exceeds this limit the Ministry of Defence will not be obliged to comply with your request. It is
therefore important that you are precise about the information you are seeking and the date or
period.

If you have any questions regarding the clarification of your request, please contact me at the address
below or by e-mail. In the meantime, your request will be held on our files.

If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this
request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain
dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the Director of Information

Exploitation, 6% Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail: Info-XD@mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Yours sincerely,

Sheffield 26 January 2007

Your letter of 8 August 2006 requested documentation relating to specific UFO sightings
on 21 January 1981, 26 April 1984, 2 February 1988 and 18 February 1988 and for any
other sightings reported in the South Yorkshire area for February 1988.

| can inform you that the MOD UFO report files for the period up to 1984 are no longer
held by the MOD so | am unabie to assist with the first two parts of your request. As you
Stated in your letter of 22 January 2007, these records have been passed to The
National Archives, Kew, Richmond, Surrey and should be available for viewing shortly.
Details of how to access information at The National Archives can be found on their

website at www.nationalarchives.gov.uk.

| attach a copy of a UFO sighting report by 2 Sheffield police Officers for 2 February
1988. Although the names the names and addresses of individuals have been removed
under exemption s.40 (Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information Act 2000, |
believe that this report (the first one in the batch) is the one you are looking for.

| have been unable to find a copy of any UFO report from West Midland police officers
for the period around 18 February 1988. However, as part of a more general search for
reports during February 1988, | have found a number of reports from around that date
that may be of interest to you.

You will, notice that information at the top and bottom of some pages has been blacked
out. This is the security classification which is now no longer required. Additionally, as
previously, mentioned, names and addresses of individuals have been removed under
exemption s.40 (Personal Information)

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the
handling of your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal
resolution is not possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an
independent internal review by contacting the Director of information Exploitation, 6”
Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail Info-XD @mod.uk). Please
note that any request for an internal review must be made within two calendar months of
the date on which the attempt to reach informal resolution has come to an end.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of
Information Act. Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate the

ase until the internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and
powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website,
http://www. informationcommissioner.gov.uk.”

Finally, | must apologise for the length of time that it has taken to answer you original
request. This was caused by a delay in retrieving the relevant files from archive, which is
deeply regretted. | hope that the delay has not been too inconvenient for you and should
like to thank you for the patience you have shown.

Yours sincerel

pre Section 40

Thank you for your request asking how many times British aircraft had been scrambled to intercept
unknown objects in UK air space over the last year.

Firstly, please accept my apologies for the delay in dealing with your request. This was due to an
administrative error caused by staff turnover. I can only hope that it has not inconvenienced you to
much. ;

I can, however, confirm that no British jets have been scrambled to intercept unknown objects in UK
airspace in the last year.

If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this
request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain
dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the Director of Information

Exploitation, 6g loor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail: Info-XD@mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 4 December 2006
requesting information relating to alleged visits of the “Aurora” hypersonic aircraft to the UK for
the period 1992-93. I shall deal with your specific requests in the order you raise them.

I am afraid that I have been unable to locate a copy of BDS/RAF/3003 dated 14 December 1992
in our files. I am in contact with the Air Attaches office in Washington to see if they have a copy
but did not wish to delay responding to you any further. If they are able to find it, I will forward it
to you at a future date.

I attach a copy of a letter dated 11 February 1993 from Hd Secretariat (Air Staff) to the US
Embassy, although it is not marked as having been copied to ACAS. However, I believe that this
may be the letter you are looking for.

You also asked for any further documentation held by the MoD on this subject. I attach what I
have been able to locate:

D/DI55/102/6 dated 1 July 1992

BDS/RAF/3003 dated 22 December 1993
D/Sec(AS)12/7(JC106/93) dated 22 April 1993

ACAS.70549 — 855 dated 27 April 1993

PQ6007 background note also covering PQ5983F and PQ5979F
PQ6859F background note also covering PQ6048

Undated Hansard page — response to Lord Kennet

As is normal, the documents have been redacted to exclude the names of individuals (other than
MPs or very senior officers) under exemption 40 (Personal Information) of the Freedom of
Information Act (2000). Security classifications at the top and bottom of pages have also been
removed, or clearly marked as unclassified.

You also wanted me to confirm that the MoD held a copy of correspondence (circa October-
December 1986) between Sec(AS) and tin to the release of a distribution

list containing DIS branch DI55. I am afraid I have been unable to locate this.

If this information does not address your requirements or you wish to complain about any aspect
of the handling of this request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance.
Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the
Director of Information Exploitation, 6th Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB.

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD
internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, .
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Finally, please accept my apologies for the delay in responding to your query.

AURORA

You may have seen an article in the 12 December 1992 edition of
Janes Defence Weekly which speculates on a hypersonic replacement
for SR71, and what has béen called Project AURORA. The Janes
article was picked up by the National press, and on the weekend
of 12/13 December, the Washington Post carried two articles on
AURORA. Secretary of the Air Force, The Honorable Donald B Rice,
was to say the least incensed by the renewed speculation, and the
implied suggestion that he had lied to Congress by stating that
AURORA did not exist. He appeared on CNN télevision to
categorically deny that there is no secret, or secretly funded,
black programme to replace the SR71.

Janes quoted sightings by-an engineer on the oil -rig Galverston
Key in the North sea in August 1989. The oil rig engineer
claimed that from below, the aircraft was roughly triangular. I
have been contacted by HQ USAF and asked what RAF aircraft it
might have been. I said that I thought it could have been
Concorde, possibly a Tornado with wings swept, or possibly the
single remaining Vulcan display aeroplane. The oil rig engineer
claims that the aircraft was refueling from a KC-135, and there
were F-111 aircraft in the formation. I have pointed out to the
Pentagon that it would certainly not have been one of our
aeroplanes because we use probe and drogue refueling,, but they
wish to cover all the options, and seem to think that as we have

- only one Vulcan which does not fly often, it could have been the

Vulcan. Secretary Rice has asked if we could say whether the
Vulcan was flying in August 1989 in the area of the Galverston
Key oil rig. _I have said this is difficuit without precise
dates, but I would contact you to see if perhaps the display

[

REDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCUMENT

pilot could be asked, in the most general terns, whether he might
have been flying in the Galverston Key oil-.rig area in August
1989. ‘ :

When I receive your reply, I. will reiterate to HQ USAF that the
st they can say is the RAF has triangular aircraft, and there
that an RAF aircraft was seen.

The Janes Defence and newspaper articles appeared after Lt Gen

emg had asked me about the supposed sighting of a stealthy
vi off Macrihanish; this sighting is not mentioned in the
articles. However, if the photograph of t hicle near

“Macrihanish is a spoof, it could be part of the AURORA saga. As
you will have gathered, the whole affair is causing considerable
irritation within HQ USAF, and any helpful comments we can make
to defuse the situation would be appreciated.

22 April 1993
ACAS —
SIGHTINGS OF UNIDENTIFIED OBJECT =)

1. 1-do not normally concern you with the UFO aspect of Sec(AS)'s work but in

the light of previous conversations we have had, you may wish to be aware of a

recent particularly unusual incidence of UFO sightings over the UK, involving

descriptions that match some of the reported characteristics of the so-called ,

“Aurora". at . |
° |

2. The sightings took place over a period of about:3 hours early on Wednesday
31 March. Most of them were in Devon and Cornwall,:South Wales and Shropshire.
There is a number of factors which make these sightings unusual; firstly, there is a
good dea! of commonality in the description of the object, and considerable
commonality in the times of many of the sightings - around 1.10 am (although
several occurred in locations some distance apart at about the same time indicating
the possibility of more than one phenomenon); ‘Secondly, none. of the usual _
explanations put forward to explain UFO. sightings seem applicable; and thirdly, the
reliability of the witnesses, a good many of whom were police officers and military
personnel. Our latest information indicates that at least seventy people witnessed
something, and | have attached a small selection of the reports.

3. My staff have made extensive efforts to find an explanation for these
sightings, including discussions with air defence and both civil and military ATC
authoritiess generally it has been confirmed that nothing unusual was seen, and no
requests for clearance for any "unusual activity" have been acknowledged to have
been received. However the ATC radar at Burrington recorded some slow moving
contacts over North Devon at the same time as some of the sightings in that area
were reported. Despite this coincidence West Drayton suggest these,recordings
could be attributed to clutter. The orily other main ATC radar (Clee Halll) which
might have picked up contacts in the area in which sightings were reported was in
fact not working on primary radar during the period of the sightings and therefore
only aircraft working Secondary Surveillance Radar could be seen. Routine checks
’ have advised us that there were no military aircraft operating at the time.

4. Some of the reports state that the object was moving at a very high speed,
while some say that it was hovering or moving very slowly. Many of the reports
refer to the object being very large, flying low and making a low humming sound.

My staff have spoken to a number of the military and police witnesses, many of
whom commented that the object was unlike anything they had ever seen before; a

&

Recycled Paper

IREDACTION ON ORIGINAL DOCU

Met Officer at RAF Shawbury reported seeing the object projecting a narrow beam of

: light at the ground.at a height of 400-500 feet and estimated its size at somewhere

between a CI30 and B747 when it passed over his head at an estimated 4000 feet.

5. In summary, there would seem to be some evidence on this occasion that an
unidentified object (or objects) of unknown origin was operating over the UK. Given

_recent speculation about Aurora by both media and MPs it is surprising that so far

this has not been taken up by the Press or, indeed, again by MPs. Frankly, | can
see little that we can do to follow it up. _ If there has been some activity of US origins

-Which is known to.a limited circle. in MOD. and is not being acknowledged it is difficult

SIGHTINGS OF UNIDENTIFIED OBJECT .

1. Thank you for your Minute at Reference which we subsequently

2. In spite of the quality of the many witnesses who reported the unusual

BACKGROUND NOTE: PQ6859F

1. This Question is the latest in a series from Mr Redmond
(and others) over the last four months which arise from
speculation about the existence of an ultra-secret hypersonic
American reconnaissance aircraft codenamed "Aurora". (Relevant

Hansard extracts and a copy of recent press articles are at

Annex A.)

2. There is no knowledge in MOD of a “black” American
programme of this nature, although it would not surprise the
relevant desk officers in the Air Staff and DIS if it did exist.
Furthermore, there have been no reports of RAF radar contacts
which might match an aircraft with the characteristics of the
so-called "Aurora". However, to answer the current Question by
saying there have been no radar contacts with "Aurora"

pre- supposes the existence of such an aircraft. Instead the
draft Answer refers back to. that given to one of Mr Redmond’s
previous Questions (Annex A/1), which was drafted to avoid any
implication that such an "Aurora" aircraft existed. The US

Embassy is content with the draft Answer.

Mr Martin Redmond (Labour)(Don Valley): To ask the Secretary of
State for Defence, how many times the radar operators at RAF
Machrihanish have detected the new United States aircraft Aurora
during the last 12 months; and if he will list by location where
it has been detected by other RAF radar monitoring stations.

DRAFT ANSWER

I have nothing to add to my Answer of 4 March 1992 (Col 220 of
the Official Report).

Sec(NATO/UK) (C)

PARLIAMENTARY QUESTION — PROJECT AURORA

1. Iam afraid that we have received yet another PQ asking
about Project Aurora (copy at Annex A). I attach for ease of
reference copies of previous Questions (Annex B).

2. We are proposing to answer the Question by referring the
MP to our Answer to PQ5983, ie the existence of any such project
would be a matter for the US authorities. However, for the
purposes of the background note we will need to answer the
Question and give Minister(AF) whatever further information we

can over and above what has been said in the background notes in
the past.

3. I should be grateful for comments from addressees, as

appropriate, by midday Friday 3 July. 1 also intend consulting
the US Embassy.

Mr John Home Robertson (Labour) (East Lothian): To ask the
Secretary of State for Defence,if he will make a statement on the
recent hypersonic flights from RAF Machrihanish; whether consent
for such flights by United States aircraft is required from his
Department; and what assessment he has made of the effects that

might be experienced by people on the ground as a result of
flights at Mach 3.

DRAFT ANSWER

There have been no such flights from RAF Machrihanish.

UNCLASSIFIED

BACKGROUND NOTE: PQ6048

1. This Question is one of four Prompted by a recent article
_in the Scotsman speculating that a secret American aircraft has
been operating around RAF Machrihanish. aA copy of the background
note supporting the draft answers to PQs 5983 and 5979 is

attached.

2. In the draft answer to PQ5979 we provided a list of all
aircraft types that have operated from RAF Machrihanish since
February 1991. None of these aircraft was capable of hypersonic
flight, which would take place at speeds in excess of Mach 5 - ie
5 (not 3) times the speed of sound. The draft answer to the
Present Question therefore reveals no information which could not
already be deduced fron the previous answer. Ministers may also.
wish to be aware that the RAF are aware of no operational
aircraft capable of hypersonic flight; a fortiori they are
certain that no such aircraft has ever operated from RAF

Machrihanish.

BACKGROUND NOTE - PQs 5983F and 5979F

1. These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third
(due for answer later this week) from George Foulkes MP have
probably been prompted by a recent article in the Scotsman
speculating that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic" aircraft code-named "Aurora", and to be the “ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft. Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article.
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. A copy ofa
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.

2. The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
that there have been no further visits to the UK. HQ3AF and the
US Embassy have also confirmed that no secret new US aircraft
has operated in Scottish airspace. The draft answer to PQ 5979F
demonstrates that no unusual aircraft (apart from an Antonov)
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.

3. There may or may not be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
it would not surprise the relevant desk officers in the Air Staff
and DIS if it did exist. The simple answer to PQ 5983F (about
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
therefore be "none” - but this pre-supposes the existence of the
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any such project (or operation) would be a
matter for the US authorities - "would be" rather than "is" to
avoid any implication that the project exists.

Mr George Foulkes (Labour)(Carrick, Cumnock and Doon Valley): To
ask the Secretary of State for Defence, if he will make a
statement on the number of unidentified supersonic radar contacts
detected in the vicinity of RAF Machrihanish since January.

DRAFT ANSWER ~

There have been no unidentified supersonic radar contacts
detected in the vicinity of RAF Machrihanish since January.

1 _KGROUND NOTE: PQ6007

1. This Question is one of four prompted by a recent article
in the Scotsman speculating that a secret American aircraft has
“been operating around RAF Machrihanish. A copy of the background

note supporting the draft answers to PQs 5983, 5979 and 6048 is
attached.

2.0 Mr Foulkes would appear to be trying to establish whether
any secret supersonic aircraft have been operating from RAF
Machrihanish, as alleged in the Scotsman article. However, his
Question asks only about unidentified radar contacts since
January and the Scotsman article refers to an alleged

unidentified contact in November 1991.

3. The draft Answer is self-explanatory.

i is

@rench firm's
new missile

Aerospatiale was due to unveil

? its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.

The ship-launched missile has

been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
from Aerospatiale’s tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tactiques).

The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
to destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.

“Our new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Aerospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua.” a company
official said.

According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km).

“In the 8-15 km gap, there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justify the
use of Exocet.”

MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
small, fast surface vessels.

The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF

AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse- |

compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.

The radar is used with the
AS 15 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.

HEADLINE NEWS

Mystery contact
~ may be Aurora

Mounting evidence suggests

that the US Government has”

secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance
aircraft, probably as a replace-
ment for the SR-7}.

A Royal Air Force air traffic
controller tracked a target
leaving the NATO-RAF base at
Machrihanish, Scotland, at an
estimated speed of Mach 3 last
November, according to The
Scotsman newspaper last week.
Another witness heard an
extremely loud jet noise near
the base around the same time.
it said.

In the USA, highly supersonic
aircraft believed to be operating
fromi Nevada have been detected
and tracked by seismological
sensors installed by the US
Geological Service (USGS). The
booms were first recorded in
June last year.

Machrihanish is one of the
most remote bases in Europe,
located near the tip of the
Kintyre peninsula in Western
Scotland. Recent base modem-
ization and a rumoured
association with the F-117

RECONNAISSANCE

BY BILL SWEETMAN _

MINNEAPOLIS

Stealth Fighter lend credence to
the new reports.

Meanwhile. the California
booms are the first substantial.
corroborated evidence of
unidentified supersonic aircraft
operating over the USA.

On at least four occasions.
sonic booms have ered on
some of the 220 sensors across
Southern California, from the
Los Angeles basin to the eastern
edge of the Mojave desert.
according to Jim Mori.a USGS
seixmoto! at the California
Institute of Technology. The
incidents were recorded in June.
October. November and late
January. The seismologists
estimate that the targets were
fying at speeds between Mach
3 and Mach 4.

So far, all the tracks have been
headed north and east over the
Los Angeles basin. pointing
directly to southern Nevada.

fs this Aurora? Artist’s impression of a Mach 6 reconnaissance aircraft
incorporating ejector ramjet engine fuelled by liquid methane or
hydrogen. Stealth would be maintained through ceramic radar absorbent
material able to withstand the fierce temperatures encountered at

hypersonic speeds (Julian Cook)

Air Force activities, including
the large flight-test base at
Groom Lake, are within the
Nellis range in Nevada. Since
the range is only 8 min from
Los Angeles at such speeds, the
targets were presumably
decelerating as they crossed the
coast.

The USGS first noticed that
its seismographs could detect
sonic booms when they
registered space shuttle landings
at Edwards AFB. California.

Mori says the wave-forms
detected in the latest incidents
are characteristic of a smaller
vehicle than the 37 m long
shuttle orbiter. Neither the
shutde nor the single SR-71B
which NASA maintains in flight
status were operating on the days
the booms were detected.

Reports that USAF is
developing hypersonic aircraft
in undisclosed ‘black’ pro-
grammes date back to the mid-
1980s. In early 1988, the New
York Times reported that a
Mach 6. stealthy reconnaissance
aircraft called Aurora was being
developed to replace the SR-71,
which was retired in early 1990.

More recently, witnesses in
Nevada and California have
reported hearing extremely loud
or ‘pulsing’ noises caused by
unidentified aircraft.

RAF MACHRIHANISH - SCOTSMAN ARTICLE

1. You asked for advice on the article in yesterday's Scotsman
which speculated that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace", near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic" aircraft code-named "Aurora" , and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft.

2. The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
that there have been no further visits to the UK.

3. There may or may not be an Aurora project. | can find no
one on the Air Staff or at desk level in the DIS who knows for
sure that such a project exists, but it would not surprise them it
it did. However, until the Americans wish to trial or operate the
new aircraft in UK airspace this is arguably a matter only for
them. If you wish to pursue this aspect further, your best course
may be to consult CDI's organisation.

4, On the question of whether a secret new aircraft has
operated in Scottish airspace, | am on firmer ground. HQ3AF and
the US Embassy advise that this aspect of the Scotsman story is
unfounded, and this is confirmed by the Air Staff. -

5. The press office have been advised not to comment on the
Scotsman article (on the grounds that if any such aircraft did
exist and had been operating in the UK we would refuse to

Mr Martin Redmond (Labour)(Don Valley): To ask the Secretary of |
State for Defence, what assistance his Department has given to
the US armed forces operation Aurora; and if he will make a
statement.

DRAFT ANSWER

The existence of any such operation would be a matter for the US
authorities.

UNCLASSIFIED

BACKGROUND NOTE - PQs 5983F and 5979F

1. These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third

: (due for answer later this week) from George Foulkes MP have

probably been prompted by a recent article in the Scotsman
speculating that “America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic” aircraft code-named “Aurora”, and to be the “ulira-
secret replacement” for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft. Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. Acopy ofa ~
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.

2. The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
that there have been no further visits to the UK. HQ3AF and the
US Embassy have also confirmed that no secret new US aircraft
has operated in Scottish airspace. The draft answer to PQ 5979F
demonstrates that no unusual aircraft (apart from an Antonov)
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.

3. There may or may not be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
it would not surprise the relevant desk officers in the Air Staff
and DIS if it did exist. The simple answer to PQ 5983F (about
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
therefore be “none” - but this pre-supposes the existence of the
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any such project (or operation) would be a
matter for the US authorities - "would be" rather than "is" to
avoid any implication that the project exists.

ao

French firm's
new missile

SINGAPORE

“Aerospatiale was due to unveil
its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.

The ship-launched missile has
been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
from Aerospatiale’s tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tactiques).

The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
To destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.

“Our new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Aerospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua.” a company
official said.

According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km),

“In the 8-15 km gap, there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justify the
use of Exocet.”

MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
smail, fast surface vessels.

The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF
AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse-
compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.

The radar is used with the
AS 15 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.

HEADLINE NEWS

Mystery c

ntact

may be Aurora

Mounting evidence suggests

that the US Government has’

secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance
aircraft, probably as a replace-
ment for the SR-71.

A Royal Air Force air traffic
controller tracked a target
leaving the NATO-RAF base at
Machrihanish, Scotland, at an
estimated speed of Mach 3 last
November, according to The
Scotsman newspaper last week.
Another witness heard an
extremely loud jet noise near
the base around the same time.
it said

In the USA. highly supersonic
aircraft believed to be operating
trom Nevada have been detected
and tracked by seismological
sensors installed by the US
Geological Service (USGS). The
booms were first recorded in
June last year,

Machrihanish is one of the
most remote bases in Europe,
located near the tip of the
Kintyre peninsula in Western
Scotland. Recent base moderm-
ization and a rumoured
association with the F-117

RECONNAISSANCE.

BY BILL SWEETMAN

MINNEAPOLIS,

Stealth Fighter lend credence to
the new reports.

Meanwhile. the California
booms are the first substantial.
corroborated evidence of
unidentified supersonic aircraft
operating over the USA.

On at feast four occasions.
sonic booms have registered on
some of the 220 sensors across
Southern California, from the
Los Angeles basin to the eastern
c of the Mojave deseri.
according to Jim Mori,a USGS

Institute of Technology. The
incidents were recorded in June.
October. November and late
January. The seismotogists
estimate that the targets were
fying at speeds between Mach
3 and Mach 4.

So far, all the tracks have been
headed north and east over the
Los Angeles basin. pointing
directly to southern Nevada,

aircraft.

BACKGROUND NOTE - PQs 5983F and 5979F =

1. These two questions from Martin Redmond MP and a third
(due for answer later this week) from George Foulkes MP have
probably been prompted by a recent article in the Scotsman
speculating that "America's most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace" near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic” aircraft code-named "Aurora", and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement" for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft. Copies of the Scotsman article and a follow-up article .
in Jane's Defence Weekly are attached at Annex A. A copy of a
minute sent to APS/Secretary of State in reply to a request for
advice on the former article is attached at Annex B.

2. The last SR-71 left the UK from RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
that there have been no further visits to the UK. HQ3AF and the
US Embassy have also confirmed that no secret new US aircraft
has operated in Scottish airspace. The draft answer to PQ 5979F
demonstrates that no unusual aircraft (apart from an Antonov)
landed at RAF Machrihanish in the past year.

3. There may or may not.be an Aurora project. There is no
knowledge in MOD of a "black" programme of this nature, although
it would not surprise the relevant desk officers in the Air Staff
and DIS if it did exist. The simple answer to PQ 5983F (about
what assistance the MOD has given to the US project) would
therefore be "none" - but this pre-supposes the existence of the
project. Nor would we be likely to be prepared to say publicly if
any MOD assistance had been given to such a project. To answer
instead that we have no knowledge of any such project is also
arguably inappropriate, since in circumstances where we were
aware we would probably not be prepared to say so. It is
therefore suggested that the question is answered by saying that
the existence of any such project (or operation) would be a
matter for the US authorities - “would be" rather than "is" to
avoid any implication that the project exists.

French firm's
new missile

“Aerospatiale was due to unveil
its MM 15 anti-ship missile at
the Asian Aerospace exhibition
in Singapore which opened on
25 February.

The ship-launched missile has
been developed in the last three
months, according to officials
from Aerospatiale’s tactical
missiles division (Engins
Tactiques).

The MM 15, derived from the
AS 15 TT air-launched weapon
system, is designed to meet the
growing demand for con-
ventional lightweight weapons
to destroy small surface targets
within the 8-15 km range.

“Our new MM 15 missile
will be offered as a direct
competitor to the British
Acrospace Dynamics ship-
launched Sea Skua.” a company
official said.

According to Aerospatiale.
the MM 15 could fill the gap
between the shorter range (8 km)
light naval artillery and long
range tactical anti-ship missiles
such as Exocet (20 km).

“In the 8-15 km gap. there is
no effective conventional
method of dealing with small
targets which do not justify the
use of Exocet.”

MM 15 has a maximum
effective range of 15 km. a
maximum speed of Mach 0.9
and weighs 103 kg with a 30 kg
warhead, says Aerospatiale. The
light weight allows operation by
small, fast surface vessels.

The MM 15 has no homing
head but is guided by a radar-
beam command-guidance tech-
nique via the Thomson-CSF
AGRION 15 (X-band, pulse-
compression) guidance radar
installed on the platform.

The radar is used with the
AS t5 TT heliborne missile
system in service with the
French, Saudi Arabian and
Bahraini navies. It- tracks both
target and missile and computes
the error in range and bearing
between them.

Mounting evidence suggests

that the US Government has’

secretly developed and deployed
a hypersonic reconnaissance
aircraft. probably as a replace-
ment for the SR-71.

A Royal Air Force air traffic
controller tracked a target
leaving the NATO-RAF base at
Machrihanish, Scotland, at an
estimated speed of Mach 3 last
November, according to The
Scotsman newspaper last week.
Another witness heard an
extremely Joud jet noise near
the base around the same time.
it said.

In the USA. highly supersonic
aircraft believed to be operating
from Nevada have been detected
and tracked by seismological
sensors installed by the US
Geological Service (USGS). The
booms were first recorded in
June last year.

Machrihanish is one of the
most remote bases in Europe,
located near the tip of the
Kintyre peninsula in Western
Scotland. Recent base modern-
ization and a rumoured
association with the F-117

HEADLINE NEWS

Mystery contact
may be Aurora

RECONNAISSANCE

BY BILL SWEETMAN

MINNEAPOLIS

Stealth Fighter lend credence to
the new reports.

Meanwhile. the California
booms are the first substantial.
corroborated evidence of
unidentified supersonic aircraft
operating over the USA.

On at least four occasions.
sonic booms have registered on
same of the 220 sensors across
Southern California. from the
Los Angeles basin to the eastern
edge of the Mojave desert.
according to Jim Mori.a USGS
seismologist. at the California
Institute of Technology. The
incidents were recorded in June,
October. November and late
January. The seismologists
estimate that the targets were
flying at speeds between Mach
3 and Mach 4.

So far, all the tracks have been
headed north and east over the
Los Angeles basin. pointing
directly to southern Nevada.
500 km away. Most secret US

incorporating ejector ramjet engine fuelled by liquid methane or
hydrogen. Stealth would be maintained through ceramic radar absorbent
material able to withstand the fierce temperatures encountered at

Air Force activities, including
the large flight-test base at
Groom Lake, are within the
Nellis range in Nevada. Since
the range is only 8 min from
Los Angeles at such speeds, the
largets were presumably
decelerating as they crossed the
coast.

The USGS first noticed that
its seismographs could detect
sonic booms when they
regisiered space shuttle landings
at Edwards AFB. California.

Mori says the wave-forms
detected in the latest incidents
are characteristic of a smaller
vehicle than the 37 m long
shuttle orbiter. Neither the
shuttle nor the single SR-71B
which NASA maintains in flight
status were operating on the days
the booms Wére detected.

Reports that USAF is
developing hypersonic aircraft
in undisclosed “black” pro-
grammes date back to the mid-
1980s. In early 1988, the New
York Times reported that @
Mach 6. stealthy reconnaissance
aircraft called Aurora was being
developed to replace the SR-71,
which was retired in early 1990.

More recently, witnesses in
Nevada and California have
reported hearing extremely loud
or ‘pulsing’ noises caused by
unidentified aircraft.

RAF MAGHRIHANISH - SCOTSMAN ARTICLE

1. You asked for advice on the article in yesterday's Scotsman
which speculated that "America’s most secret aircraft has been
operating in Scottish airspace", near RAF Machrihanish. The
aircraft is alleged to be a "very high supersonic, possibly
hypersonic” aircraft code-named "Aurora" , and to be the "ultra-
secret replacement” for the SR-71 Blackbird reconnaissance
aircraft.

2. The last SR-71 left the UK from. RAF Mildenhall in January
1990 - this is public knowledge - and HQ 3rd Air Force advise
that there have been no further visits to the UK.

3. There may or may not be an Aurora project. | can find no
one on the Air Staff or at desk level in the DIS who knows for
sure that such a project exists, but it would not surprise them if
it did. However, until the Americans wish to trial or operate the
new aircraft in UK airspace this -is arguably a matter only for
them. If you wish to pursue this aspect further, your best course
may be to consult CDI's organisation.

4, On the question of whether a secret new aircraft has
operated in Scottish airspace, | amon firmer ground. HQ3AF and
the US Embassy advise that this aspect of the Scotsman story is
unfounded, and this is confirmed by the Air Staff.

5. The press office have been advised not to comment on the
Scotsman article (on the grounds that if any such aircraft did
exist and had been operating in the UK we would refuse to

SECRET
UNCLASSIFIED

comment). HQ3AF advise that there has been no further press
interest in the article at their end.

REQUEST FOR INFORMATION

Dear IEE

Thank you for your letter 2006 in respond to my refined FOI request
of 29 August submitted to regarding the MoD file ‘DI55/108/15 Part 4
— UFO Policy.’ I will reply to your response referring to your numbered paragraphs:

your paragraph 2. Firstly as you are aware on making my initial request (dated 27 July
2006) I was asked to refine my request from the entire file to three subject areas, a
request which I took at face value in my re-submission on 29 August. I am now told ‘the
volume of information is still quite substantial’ and cannot be met within the cost limits. I
would point out that in a letter dated 23 August 2005 SERMON informed me that “this
file contains over two hundred pages” (i.e. around 100 enclosures). Please could you
explain how the release of a small percentage of this file (i.e. less than 50 pages given
your own estimates) could possibly exceed the £600 limit for processing. I would point
out that in April this year MoD/DIS approved the release of 650 pages from the UAP
Report within the same limit under one FOI request. I cannot accept how you can justify
refusing this smaller, specifically redefined request given this precedent.

Your paragraph 3. I am equally unhappy with your decision to aggregate the estimated
cost of compliance of this request with another received on 26 August on the grounds that
“{t appears that the requestors are acting in concert”. Given the fact that the existence of
this file is public knowledge and has been discussed on numerous email and internet
newsgroups, and its contents have been the subject of a number of major newspaper
articles (for example, The Guardian, ‘Is there anybody out there?’ 25 September 2006) it
is hardly surprising that other independent, interested parties wish to obtain information
from it using the FOIA, which is quite within their legal rights. This merely underlines
the public interest in the release of this file and I am surprised to find MoD, given its
commitment to the spirit of the FOIA, are reacting to this interest by finding excuses not
to fully comply with genuine requests for information.

Your paragraph 4. In response to your offer to refine my request for a second time and

specify one subject area that I am interested in, I will base my request upon the contents
of my email to ecto, 20 29 August, thusly:

I wish to request copies of papers from MoD file DIS5/108/15 Part 4 —- UFO Policy’
under the general area of “MoD correspondence concerning public access te UFO
files”.

Specifically, I wish you to include within this category any papers relating to the period

_ (circa 1985-90) when a copy of the standard MoD UFO sighting report proforma was
released to a member of the public complete with the internal distribution list containing
DSTI/DI55 addresses. :

It is my understanding that the distribution list was released in 1985-6 as a result of a
clerical error and the involvement of DI55 in providing advice to Sec(AS) on UFO
reports entered the public domain for the first time. | understand there was some
correspondence relating to this matter between FEMS) of Sec(AS)2a and a member
of the pub, Sana circa October/December 1986. I would expect this
correspondence will be filed in the DI55 file [ have requested. Therefore I wish my FOI
request to encompass this and any subsequent correspondence related to public access to
UFO files.

Finally, I wish to protest at the decision that “following a period of sixty working days
from the receipt of your next refined request [I] will be able to submit another request”.

I would point out to you that my first request was made on 27 July and I was asked to
redefine my request ~— through no fault of my own — on 29 August. On following this
advice J was given by the MoD (see my paragraph 1) I am told I have redefine my request
for a second time and the 60 day period begins from the receipt of this third request.
Surely my request should begin from 27 July, not from today’s date. I interpret this as an
unjustified attempt to delay the processing of my request still further and as a result I am
requesting an internal review of the way this matter has been dealt with.

I look forward to receiving the results of my FOI request in due course.

Yours Faithfully,

|

Further to my e-mail of 22 January 2007 regarding your Freedom of Information.
request, please find enclosed copies of UFO sightings made by, or witnessed by police officers
during the period 1987-91.

Ihave interpreted the term police rather widely to include members of any UK police service,
such as the military, transport police etc. I have divided the reports into two groups. The first, and
largest, is for reports made by police officers. The second and smaller group is for reports where
police officers are listed as witnesses.

You will notice that some of the pages are blacked out at the top and bottom of the page. This is
the security classification that must be removed before documents can be released. Additionally,
names, signatures, addresses, telephone numbers and, in the case of police officers, their numbers,
have been removed under Exemption s.40 (Personal Information) of the Freedom of Information
Act 2000.

You also asked for the names of the individuals that were involved in writing the report. This
information is also withheld under Exemption s.40.

If you are unhappy with this response or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of
your request, then you should contact me in the first instance. If informal resolution is not
possible and you are still dissatisfied then you may apply for an independent internal review by
Contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6" Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall,
SWI1A 2HB (e-mail Info-XD@mod.uk). Please note that any request for an internal review must
be made within two calendar months of the date on which the attemptto reach ch informal resolution

has come to an end. =O

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your ‘complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate the case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers, of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

http://www .informationcommissioner.gov.uk.”

Subject Station: ....... Bradford Central Date:........... 19 February 1988
UFO SIGHTING _ Sub Divisional Officer, Bradford Central

At 1930 hours on Thursday, 18 February 1988 I was off duty with
my wife and 8 year old daughter. We were travelling in my
vehicle along Toller Lane, Bradford towards Bingley.

When approximately at the junction with Leylands Lane, my
daughter remarked that she could see a star that was moving.
My wife looked at the object and said that it looked like a
line of shining white lights and was going in the same
direction as ourselves. I leant across the car and saw

the object myself. It appeared to be fairly high in the
sky. I could see a line of white lights travelling at the
usual speed of an aeroplane.

a ez . My family requested that I stop to look more closely and I
g2ei8 ° \ decided to stop in Thorne Lane off Bingley Road. This road
Fetes overlooks the whole area and judging by the speed of the
grea Pe object I thought I would get there before it did.

However, as I passed the junction with Haworth Road, my wife
who had watched the object continually, remarked that it
suddenly turned left and accelerated quickly away.

When I stopped in Thorne Lane Some seconds later the object
was nowhere in sight.

During the period whilst it was in sight I glanced at it on
three occasions. It was the size of a small passenger plane
and it was difficult to judge its shape. The line of lights
suggested it was long and thin.

I could hear no noise over the car noise. It was observed
with the naked eye and no photographs were taken.

The weather was dry and windy, there were a few clouds in the
sky that were moving quite fast with the wind. The object
flew over a housing estate the only large buildings would be
the Bradford Royal Infirmary and Sharpe's Printers.

On first seeing the object it could have been an aeroplane -
even though we could not see any coloured lights but when the
object changed direction suddenly and accelerated we were sure
it was not.

I have attached a map showing where my vehicle travelled during
the sighting. The dotted line is the approximate route taken
by the object. .

Subpitted for the information of General Administration at

, uarters.
Fd

Sunderland

D. Naked eye, binoculars, other optical device, still or movie camera.
BE. A landmark may be more useful than a badly estimated bearing.
FP. Estimated heights are unreliable. :
G. By reference to a known landmark wherever possible.

H. Changes in E, F and G may be of more use than estimates of course and
speed.
I. Moving clouds, haze, mist, etc.

marsh; river, high puildings, tall chimneys, steeples, spires, TV or

sites with flood-lights or other night lighting...

Sent: 18 December 2006 13:02 \

to:

Subject: Internet-Authorised: FOI REQUEST 18-12-2006-065847-002 - CROP CIRCLES FILE

peasection 40) |

Your Freedom of Information request of 17 December 2006 asked for the file opened by Nick Pope
on the subject of crop circles and which US agency to contact regarding a visit by General Gabriel to
Bentwaters in 1981.

In the course of handling previous requests for information about UFOs and similar phenomena,
staff in DAS have made rigorous checks of the files created and held by this branch and its
predecessors. All Government files have records showing when they were opened, closed and
destroyed (if they are not kept for the permanent record). A check of our records has shown that no
file has ever been opened on the subject of crop circles, either during Mr Popes’ tenure, or at any
other time. We are, however, unable to comment on the accuracy of anything that Mr Pope may
have said to you.

If you wish to contact the US authorities regarding the actions of any of their personnel, I would
suggest that you contact the US Department of Defence. .

If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this
request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain
dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the Director of Information

Exploitation, 6 Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail: Info-XD@mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,
http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

.

BRISTOL Date: 10 January 2006

Acknowledgement of Request for an FOI Internal Review

The Director of Information Exploitation has asked me to acknowledge your e-mail of 19
December 2006 in which you asked for an internal review of your request for information (ref: 18-
12-2006-065847-002) on 17 November 2006.

In your initial request you asked for a copy of a file that was opened by Nick Pope on crop circles
between the years of 1991-1994 and any information of military involvement with crop circles in
the last 10 years. The MOD responded to this request on 22 November 2006 and advised you
that no files held by MOD specifically related to crop circles. It was explained in the response
that any information on crop circles is filed on the UFO files. You were informed that the cost of
a search of the files for this information would exceed the appropriate cost limit, currently set at
£600.00 and MOD was therefore not obliged to comply with your request as provided for by s.12
of the FOI Act. The response went on to suggest that you refine your request to a period of one
year.

| note that you sent three e-mails to MOD on 19 December 2006. The second of those e-mails
contained a refined request for information held by MOD on crop circles in 1991. This request is
being handled by the relevant branch and you will receive a response in due course.

The internal review will look at the handling of your request and the substance of the first aspect
of your request, that is, for a file opened on crop circles by Nick Pope. On receiving a
substantive reply to your refined request, you are of course able to request an internal review of
the substance should you wish to do so, by writing to the Director of Information Exploitation, 6”
Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB.

If you remain unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not investigate the case until the internal
review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information
Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner's website, .
http:/Avww.informationcommissioner.gov.uk

The Director currently aims to complete reviews within 40 working days of receipt of the
application. Accordingly we expect to dispatch a substantive response by 16 February 2007.

Yours Sincerely

Section
Please can you confirm whether or not you have received the following request, and if
so, if any decision has yet been reached about it?

Regards,

Hello,

In the light of claims by Mr. Nicholas Pope who worked on the UFO desk
between 1991 and 1994, I wish to submit the following request on the
grounds of public interest:

All documents relating to UFOs (or any other terms with a similar (0)
meaning, eg UAP) held by the MoD produced during the tenure of Mr.
Nicholas Pope.

The public interest has been stimulated to a large extent by Mr.

Pope himself. He claims to have worked on something that he calls "The
UFO Project" for the MoD. He further claims that he investigated crop
circles, animal mutilations, and abductions in an official capacity
during his tenure. He claims a wealth of photographic and radar
evidence which he personally dealt with during his tenure. He claims
to have spent a significant proportion of his time (in the order of
50%) on the topic while he was "in charge of" "the UFO Project". He
also claims to have instigated the Condign Report by DI55.

This all appears to be inconsistent with the official position in
respect of UFOs and I feel that the only way to demonstrate the
accuracy or otherwise of his claims would be to release all of the
documents pertinent to his tenure, including those originated by DI55.

I appreciate that this is a large task, and would certainly exceed the
£600 limit stipulated under the FoIA, but it is my view that it is in
the public interest to release the documents to clear up obvious
discrepancies and ambiguities between Mr.

Pope's description of his role and the official version. I also
appreciate that at this time there is a resourcing issue within the
FoIA section of DAS, and that it would take considerable time to
provide the requested documents assuming that the release was agreed
by the MoD. It would however probably diminish the number of requests
for information pertaining to his period of tenure, thereby providing
a longer term reduction of demand on resource.

I do not see any conflict between acceding to this request and privacy
concerns relating to Mr. Pope, since Mr. Pope deliberately and
consciously courts media publicity in relation to his past role (to
the extent of describing himself as a "media tart" on occasion).

VVVVVVVVVV¥VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV VV

sure that I can provide it through a poll of members on a mail list

relevant websites operated by other interested parties. I am also
confident that I could secure the support of named television and

ewspaper journalists in such an endeavour. Please advise if you
require such evidence of public interest and I will endeavour to
provide it to you.

Regards,

AUTHORISATION FOR THE RELEASE OF INFORMATION

Applicant SSE

Case Number: 21-11-2006-133323-002 Expiry: 19 Dec 2006

The Applicant has made the following request for information:

| am writing in order to request information under the freedom of information
act files not in the public domain regarding visits from a General Gabriel
Commander-in-Chief, United States Air Forces in Europe to visit Bentwaters
early in 1981, so soon after his official visit on 3 December 1980. Why does it
seem that both Gordon Williams and Donald Moreland were unaware of the
visit?

Who handed General Gabriel the tape recordings of Charles Halt’s encounter
who testifies that a UFO landed in Rendlesham forest in 1980. Was any other
material handed over, and what subsequent analysis and investigation was
carried out by USAFE? And if the tape recording in the public domain is only
an edited extract of Halt’s recordings, what’s happened to the rest of it?

Was the DI52 offer to follow-up their initial assessment of the radiation
readings taken up? If it was, was a site visit made, and what conclusions
were reached? If the offer wasn’t followed-up, what was the reason, given the
D152 view that the readings were “significantly higher than the average
background”?

If any further information can be offered by the MOD, and if physical copies
can be purchased with regard to the information sought it would be
appreciated.

Case for release of information

All the documentation that the MoD has been able to locate on the
Rendlesham Forest incident was released in the MoD publication scheme and
is therefore available to the applicant.

The MoD has therefore fulfilled its requirement to release information. If there
are answers to the specific questions that he raises, the MoD has no further
documentation to add and therefore cannot help further.

Authorisation

aforementioned information to the Applicant, but withhold the second part.

From: EE

Sent: 22 November 2006 10:36

To:
Subject: Internet-Authorised: FREEDOM OF INFORMATION REQUEST 21-11-2006-133323-002

eu SR

Your Freedom of Information request of 21 November 2006 raised 8 questions relating to the
Rendlesham Forest incident.

The MoD Rendiesham Forest file consists of over 170 papers which were not originally on one file,
but were gathered together some time after these events. Some papers are contemporary and others
include later correspondence between members of the public and the MOD. A copy of the file was
first released to a member of the public in May 2001, following a request made under the Code of
Practice on Access to Government Information. Five papers were initially withheld under
exemptions of the Code, but these were later released on appeal.

All the documentation that the MoD has been able to identify relating to the Rendlesham incident
itself has been placed on the MoD internet site and is therefore in the public domain already. It is
now some 25 years since the alleged events and if the information you are seeking is not to be found
in the released documentation, which you have access to, I am unable to help you any further. I
would, however, suggest that if you are interested in the nature of any investigation carried out by
the USAFE that you contact the US authorities.

From: SSC

Subject: Internet-Authorised: FREEDOM OF INFORMATION ACT - CROP CIRCLES - 17-11-2006-
130847-004

Thank you for your Freedom of Information request of 17 November 2006 requesting a copy
of the file opened by Nick Pope on crop circles and your further request for information on military
involvement with crop circles in the last ten years in the county of Wiltshire.

To the best of my knowledge, the Ministry of Defence holds no files specifically related to crop
circles. There is no evidence to suggest that crop circles are caused by anything of military concern
and the MoD does not therefore investigate reported sightings or carry out any research into them.
Members of the public do sometimes associate crop circles with UFOs and write to the MoD (and
more specifically this branch) about them. No separate record is kept of them and they are filed in
the order they are received on our usual UFO files.

The only way to identify information about crop circles in Wiltshire would be to go through our files
for the period 1996-2006 manually. As we hold over 30 files for this period, the task would far
exceed the permitted £600 limit set for compliance with the Freedom of Information Act and, as
provided by Section 12 of the Act, the MoD is not obliged to comply with your request. However, if
you could express a shorter period of interest (I would suggest one year) we could conduct a limited
search of our records.

If you are unhappy with the response or wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this
request, then you should contact the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain
dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal review by contacting the Director of Information

Exploitation, oth Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A 2HB (e-mail: Info-XD@mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may take your complaint to the
Information Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act.
Please note that the Information Commissioner will not normally investigate your case until the
MOD internal review process has been completed. Further details of the role and powers of the
Information Commissioner can be found on the Commissioner’s website,

http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.

Further to my e-mail of 14 November 2006, I passed your request for information
relating to the visit of General Gabriel to RAF Bentwater/Woodbridge in 1980 to the branch that is
the focal point for dealing with US Forces in the UK.

They have conducted a search of file lists but have been unable to locate any files dealing
specifically with the Generals visit in 1980 or, a more generic visits file, covering the period you are
interested in, which is, a quarter of a century ago. It is possible that any briefing to the Commanding
Officer, if it was ever written, is on file somewhere in the Ministry of Defence archives, but the task
of identifying a single document amongst the entire MoD archive would be utterly impractical.

Whilst it is not possible to confidently identify standard working practices prevalent at the time, ] am
informed that unless the officer involved were to be meeting with members of the UK forces,
government or civil service, the MoD would not expect to receive a briefing regarding a visit by a
senior US officer to a US base in the UK.

pret section Ag |

| am writing in response to your request for a copy of the released Ministry of Defence report entitled
“Unidentified Aerial Phenomena in the UK Air Defence Region” otherwise known as the Condign Report. Your
tequest has been passed to this Department as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence responsible in conjunction with the Defence Intelligence Staff, for the release of this report.

In order to make the report available to as many individuals as possible the MOD has scanned this document
and placed it on the MOD website at:
http:/Awww.mod.uk/DefenceIntemet/FreedomOfinformation/PublicationScheme/SearchPublicationScheme/Uni:
If you do not have access to the internet at home, most public libraries have facilities for public use. The MOD
makes no charge to view, download or print this information. As the document consists of some 465 pages it
would not be an efficient use of public funds to produce and post paper copies to every individual who would
like a personal copy and your request is therefore refused in accordance with Section 21 of the Freedom of
Information Act 2000 as the information is already reasonably accessible to you by other means.

| am sorry to send what will be a disappointing reply. if you are dissatisfied with our decision to refuse
information or you wish to complain about any aspect of the handling of this request, then you should contact
the undersigned in the first instance. Should you remain dissatisfied, then you may apply for an internal
review by contacting the Director of Information Exploitation, 6'° Floor, MOD Main Building, Whitehall, SW1A
2HB. (e.mail: InfoXD @mod.uk).

If you are still unhappy following an internal review, you may be able to take your complaint to the Information
Commissioner under the provisions of Section 50 of the Freedom of Information Act. Please note that the
Information Commissioner will not investigate your case until the MOD internal review process has been
completed. Further details of the role and powers of the Information Commissioner can be found on the
Commissioner's website, http://www.informationcommissioner.gov.uk.
